=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two arrays of integers, fruits and baskets, each of length n, where fruits[i] represents the quantity of the i^th type of fruit, and baskets[j] represents the capacity of the j^th basket.
From left to right, place the fruits according to these rules:

Each fruit type must be placed in the leftmost available basket with a capacity greater than or equal to the quantity of that fruit type.
Each basket can hold only one type of fruit.
If a fruit type cannot be placed in any basket, it remains unplaced.

Return the number of fruit types that remain unplaced after all possible allocations are made.
 
Example 1:

Input: fruits = [4,2,5], baskets = [3,5,4]
Output: 1
Explanation:

fruits[0] = 4 is placed in baskets[1] = 5.
fruits[1] = 2 is placed in baskets[0] = 3.
fruits[2] = 5 cannot be placed in baskets[2] = 4.

Since one fruit type remains unplaced, we return 1.

Example 2:

Input: fruits = [3,6,1], baskets = [6,4,7]
Output: 0
Explanation:

fruits[0] = 3 is placed in baskets[0] = 6.
fruits[1] = 6 cannot be placed in baskets[1] = 4 (insufficient capacity) but can be placed in the next available basket, baskets[2] = 7.
fruits[2] = 1 is placed in baskets[1] = 4.

Since all fruits are successfully placed, we return 0.

 
Constraints:

n == fruits.length == baskets.length
1 <= n <= 10^5
1 <= fruits[i], baskets[i] <= 10^9

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def numOfUnplacedFruits(self, fruits: List[int], baskets: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Need maybe implement method with local variables for speed:
def numOfUnplacedFruits(self, fruits: List[int], baskets: List[int]) -> int:
    n = len(fruits)
    size = 1
    while size < n:
        size <<= 1
    tree = [0] * (2 * size)
    for i, cap in enumerate(baskets):
        tree[size + i] = cap
    for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
        tree[i] = tree[i << 1] if tree[i << 1] >= tree[i << 1 | 1] else tree[i << 1 | 1]
    def find_first(x):
        if tree[1] < x:
            return -1
        idx = 1
        while idx < size:
            left = idx << 1
            if tree[left] >= x:
                idx = left
            else:
                idx = left | 1
        pos = idx - size
        return pos if pos < n else -1
    def remove(pos):
        p = size + pos
        tree[p] = -1
        p >>= 1
        while p:
            left = p << 1
            tree[p] = tree[left] if tree[left] >= tree[left | 1] else tree[left | 1]
            p >>= 1
    unplaced = 0
    for x in fruits:
        pos = find_first(x)
        if pos == -1:
            unplaced += 1
        else:
            remove(pos)
    return unplaced

Let's maybe think about if there is a possible "off by one" in tree indexing. Let's write code carefully:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def numOfUnplacedFruits(self, fruits: List[int], baskets: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(fruits)
        size = 1
        while size < n:
            size <<= 1

        tree = [-1] * (2 * size)
        for i, cap in enumerate(baskets):
            tree[size + i] = cap

        for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
            left = i << 1
            right = left | 1
            tree[i] = tree[left] if tree[left] >= tree[right] else tree[right]

        def first_available(x: int) -> int:
            if tree[1] < x:
                return -1
            idx = 1
            while idx < size:
                left = idx << 1
                if tree[left] >= x:
                    idx = left
                else:
                    idx = left | 1
            pos = idx - size
            return pos if pos < n else -1

        def remove(pos: int) -> None:
            idx = size + pos
            tree[idx] = -1
            idx >>= 1
            while idx:
                left = idx << 1
                right = left | 1
                tree[idx] = tree[left] if tree[left] >= tree[right] else tree[right]
                idx >>= 1

        unplaced = 0
        for x in fruits:
            pos = first_available(x)
            if pos == -1:
                unplaced += 1
            else:
                remove(pos)
        return unplaced

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def numOfUnplacedFruits(self, fruits: List[int], baskets: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(fruits)

        size = 1
        while size < n:
            size <<= 1

        tree = [-1] * (2 * size)

        for i, cap in enumerate(baskets):
            tree[size + i] = cap

        for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
            left = i << 1
            right = left | 1
            tree[i] = tree[left] if tree[left] >= tree[right] else tree[right]

        def find_first(x: int) -> int:
            if tree[1] < x:
                return -1

            idx = 1
            while idx < size:
                left = idx << 1
                if tree[left] >= x:
                    idx = left
                else:
                    idx = left | 1

            pos = idx - size
            return pos if pos < n else -1

        def remove(pos: int) -> None:
            idx = size + pos
            tree[idx] = -1
            idx >>= 1

            while idx:
                left = idx << 1
                right = left | 1
                tree[idx] = tree[left] if tree[left] >= tree[right] else tree[right]
                idx >>= 1

        unplaced = 0

        for x in fruits:
            pos = find_first(x)
            if pos == -1:
                unplaced += 1
            else:
                remove(pos)

        return unplaced
```
